## 2026-11-14 — Phase B session deadline and recoverability design

**Review basis:** current review branch at `7a4acaeedf736f2d6b9e3692c8265190acfe1195`, current `scripts/phase-b-fixture-session-v1.mjs`, focused recovery test and `docs/phase-b-fixture-execution-review.md`. This is a design-only checkpoint: no live Firebase/Cloud requests, IAM changes, code execution, test reruns or fixture operations performed.

**Existing controls to reuse:** `LIVE_EXECUTION_ENABLED=false`, fixed project/operator and two Auth/two document identifiers, exclusive private fsynced journal v2, session UUID/provenance, create-only documents, currentDocument.updateTime conditional Firestore deletes, precise Auth UID/email/creation-time rereads, no retry of ambiguous writes, read-only `--status` reconciliation, runtime-only exact CREATE/GET/product revision PATCH and operator-only cleanup. Prior 32 combined Linux tests and four incremental offline tests must not be rerun without relevant code modifications.

**Observed risk:** a single runtime access token has lifetime 300 seconds and is minted before the full Auth/Firestore lifecycle; each runtime request times out at 5 seconds, whereas operator OAuth requests have a 20-second timeout. The runtime transport currently checks only that the token has not yet expired at request dispatch. There is no enforced whole-session deadline or reserved cleanup window, and an exception anywhere inside `runSession` closes the journal but does not attempt automatic cleanup. A token/permission/timeout failure after any create can leave an orphaned synthetic user or document. This is a safe fail-closed outcome, not proof that cleanup was performed. No 24-hour automatic cleanup mechanism exists. Auth delete lacks an atomic generation precondition.

**Proposed minimal code change (NOT implemented or authorized for live execution):**
1. Add an immutable, monotonic session deadline and a conservative reserve for operator-only exact-ID cleanup. Choose documented values after determining worst-case bounded operator calls; a nominal 120-second creation/update budget and remaining 180-second token/administrative window are *proposals*, not verified guarantees. Total duration must be measured from token issuance and must not make cleanup contingent on a still-valid runtime token. Do not silently refresh/mint additional runtime credentials or retry ambiguous requests.
2. Check token remaining lifetime *before every runtime operation* with enough buffer for its 5-second timeout and evidence journal fsync; if insufficient, STOP before that operation. Bound the whole workflow rather than only individual network requests, and do not silently skip cleanup if all mutations completed safely.
3. Before the very first write, confirm operator-authenticated exact-ID read and conditional-cleanup capabilities by approved read-only evidence/policy evaluation, check a fresh four-ID ABSENT preflight, verify exclusive journal readiness, approved cutoff and a supervised recovery operator. A read-only permission result does not conclusively prove actual DELETE capability: retain STOP and a manual per-ID recovery owner if DELETE fails.
4. Keep a mandatory explicit STOP on ambiguous create/update/delete, permission denial, journal write failure, changed receipt, incorrect identity or elapsed creation-phase deadline. Preserve local journal and use only `--status` for exact-ID read-only recovery. Never retry, infer ownership from synthetic-looking IDs, auto-delete under `UPDATE_UNKNOWN`, or re-run `--execute` to recover.
5. After safe completed writes, cleanup must independently verify the **current** revision for each exact Firestore document, session marker, owner, complete payload, original createTime and recorded updateTime, then use its exact conditional delete; cleanup the two exact Auth UIDs only after independent UID/email/creation-time reread. Verify all four ABSENT, then journal `ALL_ABSENT_VERIFIED`. If operator credential expires or a lookup/delete is ambiguous, stop and preserve residue/evidence for separately reviewed per-ID recovery. Do not claim that deadline or a 24h retention target ensures automatic deletion.
6. Add only focused offline fake-transport cases for token nearing expiry *before* a runtime mutation, total creation-phase cutoff, a partially created session timing out, journal persistence after STOP, operator cleanup not using runtime credentials, and refusal to auto-delete after `UPDATE_UNKNOWN`. Preserve all previously passing tests and the hard-disabled CLI.

**Approval boundary:** current owner approval covers the scoped staging test concept but not bypassing the hard-off source gate. A reviewed implementation, focused tests, actual Cloud Shell identity/cleanup readiness, exact immutable SHA and explicit separate approval of the precise execution command are required before any live fixture mutation. Real runtime Entity GET/CREATE/UPDATE, runtime DELETE denial and database isolation remain **UNVERIFIED**. No production/IAM/Rules/Billing/Cloud Run activation. **Production HOLD / NO-GO.**

## Phase B session deadline and cleanup review

The fixture session after credential minting now uses a monotonic deadline. Preflight and mutations share the creation budget, and operator identity checks, credential acquisition and HTTP cleanup share the fixed 90-second reserve after the creation cutoff. Each call uses the lesser of its own timeout and remaining phase time. Completion checks the deadline before recording final absence. A local timeout cannot undo a server-side mutation already in flight. Synchronous process or filesystem teardown may outlast the cutoff. Preserve the journal and reconcile exact IDs read-only after any ambiguous outcome. No automatic retries or recovery deletes.

The runtime transport still permits only exact CREATE/GET/revision PATCH. The operator independently verifies the full document, owner, session marker and receipt and uses the original verified updateTime for that revision. Auth deletes follow an exact UID/email/creation-time lookup, without an atomic UID generation precondition. Effective Entity GET/CREATE/UPDATE, runtime DELETE denial, operator cleanup permissions and database isolation remain UNVERIFIED. Configuration scope and allowlists are source evidence. The prior two tests passed on ffc2d8b; four affected offline cases passed on the new code. No cloud requests or gate changes. Production HOLD / NO-GO.

## Phase B Auth cleanup atomicity — residual risk and execution boundary

**Source review:** Current `security-hardening-review` versions of `scripts/phase-b-fixture-session-v1.mjs` and `tests/integration/phase-b-fixture-session-v1.test.mjs`. No tests rerun and no Cloud/Firebase operations performed.

Firestore document cleanup has an atomic `currentDocument.updateTime` precondition after exact path, owner, marker, payload, createTime and current receipt verification. Firebase Auth cleanup does not have an equivalent UID-generation precondition: the runner rereads exact UID/email/creationTime and then calls `accounts:delete` by UID. A concurrent delete-and-recreate of the same UID between the read and delete cannot be excluded by this API sequence. Existing tests cover a replaced account detected at reread, but do not prove race-free deletion.

**Disposition:** Do not describe Auth deletion as atomic or fully race-safe. Keep Auth cleanup as a separately supervised, explicit per-UID action with immediate identity reread, journal `DELETE_UNKNOWN` before the call, no retries, and post-delete absence verification. If identity differs, response is ambiguous, or any other actor may be changing that UID, STOP and preserve the journal for manual reconciliation. Do not weaken Firestore conditional cleanup. Do not claim all resources are absent until independently verified.

This is a residual limitation of the Auth API, not a reason to grant broader IAM permissions. No code change is justified until the owner chooses between accepting this bounded residual risk for the exact synthetic UIDs or leaving Auth fixture cleanup manual. Effective runtime Entity GET/CREATE/UPDATE, runtime DELETE denial, operator cleanup permissions and database isolation remain UNVERIFIED. `--execute` remains disabled. **Production HOLD / NO-GO.**

Auth deletion has no atomic UID-generation precondition, so this residual race risk remains explicitly acknowledged. Firestore cleanup continues to require exact provenance and the atomic `currentDocument.updateTime` precondition. Runtime Entity permissions, runtime DELETE denial, operator cleanup permissions and database isolation remain UNVERIFIED. **Production HOLD / NO-GO.**

## 2026-11-15 — P0 existing-UI marketplace session lifecycle race hardened (source-only)

**Starting point:** GitHub review HEAD `09d3ff55a0079f45c9aae4a345e24c2b990f7332`, following the exact-source offline V2 local subprocess full green CI `43705485935`. Next work shifted to highest-value independent P0 website integration instead of repeating closed hosted security tests. No Cloud Run/Firebase/IAM/Rules/billing/production access or deployment, and no modification to disabled runtime activation gates.

**Source finding:** `features/marketplace/session-integration.mjs` previously used `Promise.all(bindings.setSession(...))` without serializing multiple overlapping login/logout/session changes. When one bound UI port is slow, a stale old login can complete **after** a newer logout/identity switch and reintroduce an older private scope, even though the old promise returned false. This is a source-level race risk and is NOT a confirmed production exploit or an enabled hosted journey.

**Minimal fix:** Commit `d6a32a40cc0f23041274672a1f4a8df470d663ca` queues fanout tasks to existing Product/Store/optional Provider/Inbox bindings, immediately invalidates the old session snapshot and calls each binding's existing `changeScope` cancellation on all new login/logout/invalid-session actions, skips superseded queued logins before dispatch, and rolls back any partial failed fanout with `setSession(null)`. Only approved `uid` and `sessionId` are forwarded, never raw tokens/roles. This uses existing ports, no new Auth listener, data schema, endpoint, IAM or dependency.

**Regression:** Commit `75ad9d3dd596a2c6723d7835be98864d6bde09d7` adds three deterministic, no-network cases to `tests/integration/marketplace-session.test.mjs`: (1) a blocked first login followed by account switch and logout cannot reanimate the old session; (2) new identity wins when old async fanout completes late; (3) invalid latest identity skips prior pending private work and never forwards secret metadata. Existing same-session and failure-rollback cases remain. The repo's `test:provider-draft:transaction` script already includes this exact test file, and the review Safety CI runs that script; therefore no new CI step or redundant test workflow was added.

**CI status when handoff initially written:** GitHub Actions exact-code safety run `43706388021` for `75ad9d3d` was in progress and no full PASS may be claimed until completed; companion provider emulator run `43706388078` also in progress then. On a failed test, inspect the failing step and perform a minimal source-only repair rather than a broad rewrite. No prior passing suite need be rerun.

**Next:** verify the exact-code full CI and independent emulator result once; then continue the remaining P0 Product/Store/Provider UI, mandatory-media, tri-language and device acceptance gates without calling source-only completion a live-ready website. No active backend rebind, staging Rules or production changes without a separate exact owner grant. **STAGING HOSTED NO-GO; STAGING RELEASE NO-GO; PRODUCTION HOLD/NO-GO**.

The current identity snapshot invalidates synchronously at the start of each lifecycle change; existing bindings get `changeScope` cancellation, and serialized fanout/rollback prevents out-of-order final state. Scope validation and no-token/role-forwarding contracts are preserved. This is source+offline/browser/emulator verification, **NOT** proof of live identity revocation, production login/permissions or full launch readiness.

**Official source verification without cloud access:** Google Firestore database guide https://firebase.google.com/docs/firestore/manage-databases documents `resource.name == "projects/oldx-staging/databases/(default)"` as a valid database-condition pattern AND explicitly warns Console access is not enforced by these per-database IAM conditions: effective permissions require REST/client-library checks. Official Firestore IAM mapping https://firebase.google.com/docs/firestore/security/iam confirms `datastore.databases.get` is a transaction begin/rollback, whereas database metadata GET requires `datastore.databases.getMetadata`, not included in OLDX's four-permission role. Do not install Debian unstable packages, remove essential shared libs, or claim a CVE fixed/accepted.

This is reliable evidence of vulnerable *source package version families* in Google's currently locked upstream base recipe, **NOT** a new signed scan proving exact prior 6abf image package content or live reachability. Distroless SECURITY.md tracks Debian and directs users to wait on Debian's release channel for unpatched Debian13 CVEs. This proves a vendor patch for a different distro, **not** a Debian13 fixed version and **not** permission to transplant Ubuntu libc6.
